TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 13, 2015 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  Past 
practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this part 
of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not be 
seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the nomination.)  
Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

     4. C.    01.13.15 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into 
the hands of Rick West, Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in 
or coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the  
following depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
First Merit Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick 
West, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
or the Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund 
account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belongi ng to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The 
Troy Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next 
Generation Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the 
Treasurer, Rick West, and 
11
     4. C.    01.13.15 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment 
authorized by subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above.

Oakland in the State of Michigan this 13th day of January 2015.

      7. A.   01.13.15 
7.  RECOGNITION       
A.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who are 
tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the  
opportunities for our students and our families . Tonight, we would 
like to recognize our Board of Education  members with a gift  that 
represents our students and staff, the very people work hard to 
serve.  
The gift before you is a token of appreciation that will allow Board 
members to enjoy some of the talents that our students bring to the 
Troy School District, under the direction  of some very dedicated fine 
arts teachers. Before you is a CD of music from around the district —
some choir performances, some orchestra and band and some of our 
fall theatrical productions. We hope that this will serve as a reminder 
of the sincere appreci ation of your support of our Fine Arts 
programs. You are truly a group that has given so much to our 
students and we are grateful to have each and every one of you. 
Thank you for all you do.   
Recommended Action:  Present gift to Board members. 
18
     7. B.   01.13.15 
7.   RECOGNITION  
B.   TSD Mission Moment—Athens High School Urban Farming Initiative  
The Pingree 4-H program is an urban farming initiative that gives students in the 
Troy School District the opportunity to raise animals for competition in the Oakland 
County Fair. The program, under the leadership of teacher Christina Harlan, brings 
together special education and general education students from Athens High School 
and Baker and Larson Middle Schools. The farm is located in the City of Detroit, and 
every Friday through the school year, students, parent volunteers and Miss Harlan 
gather to feed, clean and care for their assigned animal. 
The students then show the mini -cows, horses, goats, chickens and a variety of 
other animals a t the Oakland County Fair in the summer. Last year, they brought 
home many awards, but more than that, learned valuable lessons about farming, 
responsibility, independence and the teamwork it takes to run a farm effectively.  
We would like to invite teacher Christina Harlan and her 4-H students up to receive 
their certificates.  
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Invite participants up and present certificates.

7. RECOGNITION        7. C.      01.13.15 
C.  MEMSPA Outstanding Practicing Principal 
The Troy School District is proud to recognize Bemis Elementary Principal Jereme y 
Whan, who was named “ MEMSPA Outstanding Practicing Principal ” by the 
Michigan Elementary and Middle School Principals Association, or MEMSPA. Mr. 
Whan is a dedicated and exemplary educational leader who was nominated by a 
committee made up of several Bemis teachers and a former member of his staff 
when he served as a principal in West Bloomfield. This award recognizes his 
accomplishment and level of service to his school, his staff and most importantly, 
his students.  
Mr. Whan was surprised with the news of this honor w hile attending the annual 
MEMSPA conference. His family was able to be with him at that time, and his wife 
is here with him this evening. 
At this time, I would like to invite, Paul Liabenow, Executive Director of MEM SPA, 
to come to the podium to tell us a little bit more about the award and r ecognize 
Mr. Whan. 
Recommended Action:   Mr. Liabenow will introduce Jeremey Whan, who 
will be invited to say a few words

8. A.   01.13.15
BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING     MINUTES   DECEMBER 2, 2014 
       SERVICES BUILDING  
  President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7:00 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Dr.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members  Edmunds,  Fleming,  Hauff,  Miletti,  Schmidt, 
and  Underwood.    Also  present  were  Superintendent 
Machesky;  administrators  Birmingham,  Brasington, 
Dziatczak,  Fulcher,  Keeling,  Whan,  West,  Witt,  and 
Young  and  approximately  150  additional  members  of 
the audience.   
ADDITIONS AND DELETIONS  None.  
(AGENDA ITEMS) 
RECOGNITION 
TSD  Mission  Moment  –  Smith 
Connectors 
Tim Fulcher, Smith principal, invited Ray Torres and Jen 
Hartenburg,  the  advisors,  to  introduce  the  Smith 
Connectors.   The  Smith  Connectors  is  a  mentoring 
program that consists of 8th grade students who serve as 
leaders  at  Smith  Middle  School  as  well  as  in  the 
community.  The  Smith  Connectors  are  held  to  a  high 
expectation  of  leadership,  respect,  and  responsibility.  
Their actions and choices both in and out of school are 
expected  to  uphold  the  standards  of  Smith.   The 
Connectors  are  involved  in  many  leadership  activities 
throughout  the  school  year  such  as  assisting  at 
Adventure  Island,  helping  6th  graders  adjust  to  middle 
school,  teambuilding  and  anti‐bullying  activities.   They 
help to create a positive and safe culture at Smith.  They 
also  provide  tours  to  5th  graders  who  will  be  entering 
middle  school  the  following  year  and  participate  in 
various  community  service  projects.   These  8th  grade 
students  are  role  models  for  the  other  students  at 
Smith.  The Board shared comments and asked students 
22
PUBLIC COMMUNICATION 
None. 
a  couple  of  questions  as  to  Mr.  Fulcher  announced  the 
45  Smith  students  who  serve  as  Connectors.   Each 
student  received  a  certificate  of  recognition  for  their 
participation in the group.   
National  Blue  Ribbon  Schools  – 
Bemis  and  Wattles  Elementary 
Schools 
Bemis  Elementary  School  at  this  evening’s  meeting  for 
their  designation  as  National  Blue  Ribbon  Schools.  This 
award  honors  them  as  one  of  the  very  best  schools  in 
the  Unites  States.   Secretary  Arne  Duncan  recognized 
Wattles  and  Bemis  in  a  ceremony  in  Washington,  D.  C. 
during  the  month  of  November.  This  year,  only  337 
schools from around the country were chosen to receive 
this  honor.  This  award  affirms  the  hard  work  of 
students,  staff,  parents,  and  the  Bemis  and  Wattles 
communities.   As  Secretary  Duncan  stated,  “National 
Blue Ribbon Schools are models of consistent excellence 
and  a  resource  for  other  schools  and  districts.   We 
celebrate  them  for  their  tireless  effort  and  boundless 
creativity  in  reaching  and  teaching  every  student.” 
Jeremey  Whan  and  Joyce  Brasington  were  asked  to 
come  up  and  receive  a  certificate  in  recognition  of  this 
award.   Retired Wattles principal, Dr. Judy Garrett, was 
also present and recognized at this time. 
Dr. Machesky shared that both schools held events with 
staff,  students  and  community  to  celebrate  their 
achievement.

Plante Moran Report on  
District’s  Audit  Report  for  year  ended  June  30,  2014.  
She  reviewed  governmental  revenue  and  expenditures, 
general  fund  revenue  and  expenditures,  instructional 
expenditures  comparison  to  Oakland  County  Schools,

of  the  fund  balance  as  a  percentage  of  expenditures.

detailed  review  with  Plante  Moran  representatives  at 
the November 18, 2014 Board Workshop.

funding  and  the  uncertainty  in  current  legislation  as  to 
the future of funding for public education.

B. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105 - Elementary  
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend  to implement a Schools of 
Choice program for non -resident students who attend another school 
district within Oakland County.  It would be appropriate, for the 2015-16 
school year, for the Board of Education to consider adopting a resolution 
for Schools of  Choice Section 105 for 100 kindergarten and 50 first 
grade non-resident students who attend a school district within Oakland 
County (total students includ e non Oakland County residents  – section 
105c). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School  Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for the 201 5-2016 school year; for 100 non-
resident kindergarten and 50 non-resident first grade students  who attend a 
school district within Oakland County (total students include non Oakland County 
residents – section 105c).

DRAFT TSD COMMUNICATION PLAN 
2014/2015   
GOAL: The goal of this plan is to enhance our communication methods, vehicles and avenues to 
reach the maximum number of stakeholders within the Troy School District as well as the greater 
Troy community and beyond in a thoughtful, clear and efficient manner. Doing so will help 
position TSD going forward as a lighthouse district both nationally and internationally as well as 
boost internal and external communication. 
BACKGROUND: The TSD has countless great stories to tell, from student achievement to 
outstanding staff and programming initiatives. We are the “success story” of exemplary U.S. 
public education and our students, employees and citizens would all see tangible benefits in 
further promoting that success. It is also important that the District share critical information 
regarding budgeting, legislative impact and strategic planning to better engage our community in 
the process. This plan will allow us to anticipate upcoming issues and prepare people with 
necessary background on key areas before crises arise.  
The plan would also “introduce” the new Superintendent, Dr. Richard Machesky, and provide 
the opportunity for fresh methods of communication (both one-way and two-way), which would 
enhance the relationship between the superintendent’s office and various stakeholders, including 
staff, parents and students.  
EXISTING COMMUNICATION TOOLS: 
 School Messenger: This tool is an extremely effective way to reach parents, students and 
staff for both immediate/critical communications as well as e-blasts, newsletters, etc. The 
downside is that it ONLY reaches our TSD community. We pay per student and cannot 
add additional community members who wish to keep up to date on the district, so we 
post all newsletters on the TSD website for public consumption. 
 Social Media: The number of fans on the TSD Facebook page has increased more than 
100% and the number of Twitter followers has more than tripled in the past 12 months. 
The users who visit, comment and retweet are more engaged and active than any time in 
the last five years. Continuing to build and develop these audiences is important to 
reaching a key demographic that may not regularly read email or are inundates from 
messages using more “traditional” means.  
 TSD Website: The TSD website and individual school websites provide both static 
information (school start/end times, registration information, building contact 
information) and “news items” (updated consistently during the school year, less 
151
frequently during the summer months.) As measured by page hits, this is the go-to 
vehicle for items such as the lunch menus, email addresses and stationary information, 
but is utilized less so for news and events. Proposed change: Replace “News” section 
with Key Information/Links Section and embed a twitter feed along the right side of the 
front page (where the “links” currently reside) where people can see the most up-to-date 
information, without adding to the work load by duplicating efforts. Photo gallery on the 
front page would remain because it provides visual interest and feedback tells us 
parents/staff like to see student photos prominently displayed. A new content 
management system and website overhaul is being considered for the 2015-16 school 
year.   
 TSD E-blasts: This is a bi-weekly e-newsletter that lists events, activities and 
achievements by students and staff. Although helpful in disseminating information 
(especially in regards to student fundraisers), this vehicle takes a tremendous amount of 
time for limited readership (the “list” format does not lend itself to a visually friendly 
read.) Proposed change: Sending out link to online version of School Life (see below) 
and encouraging TSD families to connect with us electronically on Twitter and 
Facebook.    
 Troy Times Staff Newsletter: This vehicle is well read and consistently used by 
employees to get the word out regarding items for sale, births and staff-only discounts 
and offers.  
 Vimeo: This is our video-hosting site for videos produced by TS-TV. It allows us to put 
hours of sporting events, fine arts performances and classroom videos online for a very 
low costs. We send links out to parents and have a direct link on the TSD website. 
 Superintendent Welcome: This has traditionally been a one-time video message sent out 
to welcome staff back in August. Proposed change: Regular, monthly podcasts produced 
by TS-TV on subjects of importance to TSD staff and community (student testing, budget 
issues, legislative issues, school safety—as well as current events.) See below. 
 Earned Media: The TSD maintains an excellent relationship with local newspaper, radio, 
TV and online media. Continuing to cultivate that strong connection and rapport is 
critical to credibility and the opportunity to help shape external messaging. 
 Paid Media: The TSD buys a limited amount of ad space in weekly, daily and monthly 
publications to advertise for Schools of Choice (K-2, Baker Middle School and IA East.) 
Radio spots are occasionally purchased for IA East as well. The strategic placement of ad 
dollars has been extremely successful in bringing in applications for SOC. 
 Personal visits/meetings: Arguably one of the most important communication vehicles. 
The Superintendent would maintain a calendar of visits to PTO/PTA meetings, 
community/civic organizations, staff meetings, athletic and fine arts events, etc. 
NEW COMMUNICATION TOOLS/INITIATIVES: 
152
 School Life newspaper: This joint project with School Life publications will provide our 
parents, students, staff and the greater Troy community with a professionally done, hard 
copy monthly newspaper filled with great stories about the Troy School District, as well 
as a compiled district calendar, menus and photo pages. Every student in the TSD would 
be given a copy to take home and newsstands would be placed around Troy for 
community members to pick up a free copy. This is produces at no cost to the TSD (it is 
advertiser supported) though the communications department will be busy editing copy 
and proofing stories before they hit print. 
 Superintendent’s Column: This is a monthly “Message from the Superintendent” that 
would appear in each issue of School Life. The topic/content could directly coincide with 
the monthly podcast or occasionally be completely unique. Some columns would reflect 
directly the content of that month’s School Life (i.e. Dr. Machesky could comment on 
achievements covered in the newspaper.) 
 Superintendent’s Podcasts: These would be monthly video messages produced by TS-
TV on subjects of importance to TSD staff and community (student testing, budget 
issues, legislative issues, school safety—as well as current events.) The format would be 
Q & A with students from various broadcasting classes serving as the interviewer for 
each podcast. Topics would follow a calendar (listed below) that allows for substitution 
and/or additional podcasts if current events dictate a need. 
 OneTroy Doing More Videos (working title): These videos would highlight a 
contribution an individual staff member (or group of staff) is doing that inspires, teaches, 
reaches and nurtures students in an extraordinary way. These videos would also show the 
power of our collective work and the impact it has on our students. They would be 
loosely modeled after this videos, produced by the Ottawa Area Schools: 
http://doingmoretogether.org/stories/  
 Weekly Staff Emails from Superintendent: These are simple, straightforward, casual and 
conversational—just a message each week from Dr. Machesky on something that struck 
him in a building visit, a word of thanks, a congratulations and reminder that we all work 
in a phenomenal visit because of the people who make TSD great. Scheduled to go out 
either every Friday or Monday. 
153

13.  OTHER            
B.  TSD Advocacy Initiative  
Strong schools build  strong communit ies and nowhere is that more 
evident that in the Troy School District. Maintaining our world class 
schools is a priority for the Board and the District. Decisions are made at 
the state and federal level that have a direct impact on our students and 
it’s important that our collective voice  is heard in these discussions. We 
are committed to focusing the combined energy of all stakeholders on 
maintaining our exemplary schools and will work together to advocate and 
do the “right work” for Troy students.  
Kerry Birmingham, Director of Communications and Strategic Initiatives, is 
here to give a brief report on the efforts to bring our community together 
in advocacy for public education in Troy.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report will be given by Kerry Birmingham, Director of 
Communications and Strategic Initiatives.
